⏺ Phase 1 audit: not ready to train

  I audited all 8 sources (46,238 images), and I didn't change anything in
  data/raw/. The only recently modified files in there are macOS .DS_Store
  files, written by Finder at 15:53–16:25, before the audit ran. The full report
  is in 01_audit_report.md (reports/01_audit_report.md).

  Verdict: Can I train now? No.

  The vehicle class has 0 labelled images: none of the 8 sources boxes vehicles,
  even where they're clearly visible. People are also left unboxed in 20–100%
  of images in 7 of the 8 sources. Everything else is fixable in Phase 3 once
  you've answered the decisions below. The other classes are well above the
  200-image target:

  ┌────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┐
  │       Class        │ Distinct images (estimate) │
  ├────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ person             │ ~3,700                     │
  ├────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ weapon (guns only) │ ~3,500                     │
  ├────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ fire_smoke         │ ~4,500                     │
  ├────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ vehicle            │ 0                          │
  └────────────────────┴────────────────────────────┘

  Top 5 problems and fixes

  #: 1
  Problem: No vehicle boxes in any source. On a 30-image sample per source,
  pretrained YOLO found vehicles in 23–83% of images in 6 sources (7% in
  weapon, 0% in entry).
  Fix: Auto-label vehicles with yolo11s, then you review. Rough guess:
  ~1,500–2,000 vehicle images after that.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 2
  Problem: People left unboxed: loitering 100% of sampled images, tampering_1
  90%, entry 63%, the fire sources 20–27%. Only weapon__roboflow labels people
  properly.
  Fix: Auto-label missing people, then you review.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 3
  Problem: Augmented copies: 4 sources were exported with 3–5 copies of every
  image (flips, rotations, noise). The 46,238 images come from only about
  15,000 originals.
  Fix: Keep one copy per original (seed 42). Augmentation happens during
  training.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 4
  Problem: Leakage in the existing splits: for example, all 25 video clips in
  tampering_1 sit in more than one split. There are 128 sets of exact
  duplicates, 497 near-duplicates shared between entry and weapon, and one
  video clip with 7,466 frames.
  Fix: Ignore the old splits and re-split 70/15/15 group-aware. Cap frames per
  clip.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 5
  Problem: Wrong content and missing labels: entry__roboflow_restricted has no
  entry content. It's guns, knives and cigarettes, with no vehicles. 3,872 of
  its images have no label file, though each has a labelled augmented copy.
  Some fire images are off-topic (a birthday candle, synthetic smoke).
  Fix: Keep its Gun images and exclude the 3,872 unlabelled ones. I'll list
  off-topic images for you to check.

  Decisions needed (recommended answer in bold)

  1. Knives and swords → weapon or drop? Drop for now, and leave out images that
     only contain blades. Workshop staff handle knives and tools every day, so
     including them would trigger many false alerts.
  2. Smoking (cigarettes) → drop, and leave out images that only contain it?
     Yes.
  3. Theft mask → drop? Yes. The person wearing it already has a Person box.
  4. Fire and smoke → one fire_smoke class? Yes.
  5. Behaviour boxes:
     - theft and the 6 whole-body loitering classes → person. Yes.
     - hiding_face1 (face-sized boxes) → drop. Yes.
     - stealing (the box covers the car plus the person) → drop. Yes.
  6. Three-wheelers and bicycles? Three-wheeler → vehicle, bicycle → drop. The
     pretrained model can't detect three-wheelers, so these would need hand
     labels.
  7. Auto pre-label missing people and vehicles (marked auto, then you review)?
     Yes. It's the only way to get a vehicle class from these sources.
  8. Keep one copy per original in the 4 augmented sources? Yes.
  9. Caps: at most 150 frames per video clip, and no class more than 5× the
     smallest? Yes.
  10. Exclude the 3,872 unlabelled entry images, plus off-topic images after you
      check them? Yes.
  11. Use short source ids in file names, since the raw folders can't be
      renamed? Yes. Three fire folders don't follow the naming pattern, and
      loitering__kaggle_parking is really from Roboflow.
  12. Keep tampering__roboflow_car_1 ("Private" licence) and
      fire_vehicel_roboflow_fire-smoke_2 (unknown licence, probably FireNET)?
      Yes, for the research prototype.
  13. Use the 628 empty fire CCTV frames as background, but only after
      auto-labelling? Yes. The 494 empty loitering frames show people, so they
      aren't background.
  14. Add your own photos of service-centre bays? Yes. They're the closest match
      to real use and will be kept private.

  What I wrote

  - Reports (in reports/):
    - 01_audit_report.md: the main report.
    - 01_audit_tables.md: the full numeric tables.
    - 01_audit_details.csv: one row per image.
    - 01_audit_stats.json, 01_partial_label_sample.csv,
      01_partial_label_summary.json: the data behind the tables and the YOLO
      check.
  - Config and docs: configs/class_map.draft.yaml (values starting with ? wait
    for your decision) and docs/sources.md.
  - Scripts: scripts/01a–01d and audit_common.py. They can be re-run and use
    seed 42.
  - Model: models/yolo11s.pt, downloaded free.
  - Sample sheets: the 10-image sheets I used to judge content are in the
    session scratchpad only, not in the project.

  The "originals" and vehicle numbers above are estimates; the vehicle figure
  comes from 30-image samples per source. docs/decision_log.md stays empty until
  you answer the questions.

      112  ## 6. Decisions needed
      113  
      107 -1. **Knives and swords → `weapon` or `drop`?**                
      108 -   *Recommended: `drop` for this version, and leave out images
          - whose only labels are knives/swords.*                        
      109 -   Reason: staff in a service centre handle knives, cutters an
          -d long tools every day → many false weapon alerts;            
      110 -   the knife data is mostly studio stock photos. Including the
          -m would add ~3,000 originals to `weapon`.                     
      111 -2. **`Smoking` (cigarettes) → drop, and leave out Smoking-only
          - images?** *Recommended: yes* (cigarette smoke is not a fire; 
          -no vehicle context).                                          
      112 -3. **`Theft mask` → drop?** *Recommended: yes* (the masked per
          -son already has a `Person` box).                              
      113 -4. **Fire and smoke → one `fire_smoke` class (all 4 fire-carry
          -ing sources)?** *Recommended: yes.*                           
      114 -5. **Behaviour boxes:** theft and the 6 whole-body loitering c
          -lasses → `person`; `hiding_face1` and `stealing` → `drop`?    
      115 -   *Recommended: yes, as listed.* Missing people are then adde
          -d by auto pre-labelling.                                      
      116 -6. **Three-wheelers and bicycles → `vehicle`?** *Recommended: 
          -three-wheeler → `vehicle` (common in Sri Lankan service centre
          -s), bicycle → `drop`.*                                        
      117 -   Note: the pretrained COCO model has no three-wheeler class,
          - so these must be boxed by hand.                              
      118 -7. **Auto pre-label missing `person` and `vehicle` boxes (yolo
          -11s, conf 0.40), marked `auto`, then you review?**            
      119 -   *Recommended: yes* — it is the only way to get a `vehicle` 
          -class from these sources.                                     
      120 -8. **Keep one copy per original in pre-augmented sources** (en
          -try, fire_smoke, fire_vehicle_1, tampering_2)? *Recommended: y
          -es* (random copy, seed 42).                                   
      121 -9. **Caps:** at most 150 evenly spaced frames per video clip, 
          -and cap every class at 5× the smallest class (random by group,
          - seed 42)?                                                    
      122 -   *Recommended: yes.* The one-clip `mgd_custom` group alone h
          -as 7,466 frames.                                              
      123 -10. **Exclusions:** the 3,872 entry images with no label file 
          -(exclude); obvious off-topic images I list for you

⏺ Phase 1 audit (re-run): still not ready to train

  Since the first audit you'd added two sources, Automotive.v2i.yolov11 and
  Knife Detection.v2i.yolov11, so I re-ran the whole audit on all 10 sources
  (58,704 images). Nothing in data/raw/ was changed. The full report is in
  01_audit_report.md (reports/01_audit_report.md).

  Can I train now? No.

  The Automotive source fixes the worst gap: vehicle now has about 1,470
  labelled original images. "Originals" means one copy per picture, after
  removing augmented copies. The data still isn't usable as it stands: most
  sources leave people and vehicles unboxed, all vehicle labels come from just
  12 videos, many sources contain augmented copies, and the old splits leak.
  Every class clears the 200-image target. After Phase 3, I expect Yes, with
  warnings, mainly because the auto labels won't have been reviewed yet.

  ┌──────────────────────────┬───────────┬───────────────────────────┐
  │          Class           │ Originals │ Distinct groups (variety) │
  ├──────────────────────────┼───────────┼───────────────────────────┤
  │ person                   │ 4,857     │ 3,248                     │
  ├──────────────────────────┼───────────┼───────────────────────────┤
  │ vehicle                  │ 1,472     │ 12 (all from 12 videos)   │
  ├──────────────────────────┼───────────┼───────────────────────────┤
  │ weapon (guns and rifles) │ 7,742     │ 4,422                     │
  ├──────────────────────────┼───────────┼───────────────────────────┤
  │ fire_smoke               │ 4,466     │ 3,159                     │
  └──────────────────────────┴───────────┴───────────────────────────┘

  The largest class has about 5.3× as many images as the smallest (weapon vs
  vehicle), just over the 5× warning level.

  Top 5 problems and fixes

  #: 1
  Problem: People and vehicles left unboxed. Vehicles are visible but unboxed in

  23–83% of fire and tampering images, and in about 23% of Automotive images.
  People are unboxed in 20–100% of images in 8 of 10 sources.
  Fix: Auto-label the missing boxes with yolo11s, mark them auto, then you
  review.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 2
  Problem: Vehicle data is narrow. All vehicle boxes come from 12 phone videos
  of
  moving city traffic: no parked cars, no service bays, no CCTV angle.
  Fix: Add auto-labelled vehicles from the fire and tampering sources, cap
  frames
  per video, and add your own bay photos.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 3
  Problem: Augmented copies. Five sources contain extra copies of the same
  pictures. This includes Automotive, whose README says "no augmentation" but
  whose train split holds 5 copies of most images. 58,704 images come from only
   ~23,900 originals.
  Fix: Keep one copy per original (seed 42).
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 4
  Problem: Leakage. All 25 clips in tampering_1 and 6 of the 12 Automotive
  videos
  sit in more than one split. 3,574 Knife Detection images are near-duplicates
  of entry images, and one gun video has 7,466 frames.
  Fix: Ignore the old splits. Re-split group-aware, cutting long videos into
  blocks.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 5
  Problem: Unlabelled and off-topic images. 3,872 entry images have no label
  file. Much of the weapon data is catalogue or stock photos, plus a cigarette
  collage and a birthday candle labelled as fire.
  Fix: Exclude the unlabelled images. I'll list off-topic images for you to
  check
  in Phase 3.

  Decisions needed (recommended answer in bold)

  1. Knives and swords → weapon? Drop for now, and leave out images that only
     contain blades. Workshop staff use blades every day, so they'd cause false
     alerts, and including them would add about 6,200 originals to weapon.
  2. Smoking (cigarettes) → drop, and leave out images that only contain it?
     Yes.
  3. Theft mask → drop? Yes.
  4. Fire and smoke → one fire_smoke class? Yes.
  5. Behaviour boxes:
     - theft and the 6 whole-body loitering classes → person. Yes.
     - hiding_face1 → drop. Yes.
     - stealing → drop. Yes.
  6. Vehicle types:
     - Three-wheelers → vehicle. Yes.
     - Automotive other-vehicle → vehicle. Yes. It covers auto-rickshaws, but
       also cycle-rickshaws and carts.
     - Bicycles → drop. Yes.
  7. Auto-label missing people and vehicles, then you review? Yes.
  8. Keep one copy per original in the 5 augmented sources? Yes.
  9. Caps: at most 150 frames per video, cut long videos into blocks of
     consecutive frames, and no class more than 5× the smallest? Yes.
  10. Exclude the 3,872 unlabelled entry images and the off-topic images you
      confirm? Yes.
  11. Use short source ids in file names (for example vehicle__rf_automotive and
      weapon__rf_knife-detection)? Yes. I also need the threat types for the
      two new folders: Automotive = normal, Knife Detection = weapon. Their
      names don't follow the naming pattern and contain dots or spaces.
  12. Keep the "Private" and unknown-licence sources for research use? Yes.
  13. Use empty fire CCTV frames as background, but only after auto-labelling
      (target 5–10% of train)? Yes.
  14. Add your own photos of service-centre bays? Yes. There are currently no
      parked-car images at all.

  What changed in this run

  - Every report and config file was regenerated for all 10 sources:
    reports/01_*, configs/class_map.draft.yaml and docs/sources.md.
  - I fixed two problems in the scan and table scripts:
    - Phone-video frames named like IMG_0006-0529 are now grouped by video.
      Before this, the Automotive videos weren't recognised as clips.
    - Class counts are now checked against originals, not raw image totals.

  The "originals" counts and the vehicle shares are estimates; the shares come
  from 30-image samples per source. docs/decision_log.md will be filled in once
  you answer.

  data/raw/ had been completely replaced since yesterday's audit. All folders
  are renamed or re-exported, Automotive and Knife Detection are gone, and Fire
  And Smoke Detection…6k is new. I re-audited what's there now: 9 sources and
  50,656 images, which come from about 22,200 originals once augmented copies
  are removed. Nothing in data/raw/ was changed. The full report is in
  01_audit_report.md (reports/01_audit_report.md).

  Can I train now? No.

  vehicle is back to 0 labelled images, because none of the 9 sources boxes
  vehicles. The other classes clear the 200-image target easily:

  ┌────────────────────┬───────────┐
  │       Class        │ Originals │
  ├────────────────────┼───────────┤
  │ person             │ 3,663     │
  ├────────────────────┼───────────┤
  │ weapon (guns only) │ 3,596     │
  ├────────────────────┼───────────┤
  │ fire_smoke         │ 9,569     │
  ├────────────────────┼───────────┤
  │ vehicle            │ 0         │
  └────────────────────┴───────────┘

  Top 5 problems and fixes

  #: 1
  Problem: No vehicle boxes. On a 30-image sample per source, pretrained YOLO
  found vehicles in 10–83% of images in 8 of 9 sources, but none are labelled.
  Fix: Auto-label vehicles, then you review (rough guess: 2,000–3,500 images,
  many of burning or night-IR cars). Better still, put a vehicle-labelled set
  back, such as Automotive, and add your own bay photos.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 2
  Problem: People left unboxed: Surveillance 100% of sampled images, theft 90%,
  Suspicious Activity 43%, the fire sets 20–30%. Only cctv security labels
  people properly.
  Fix: Auto-label missing people, then you review.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 3
  Problem: Augmented copies: 5 sources contain 3× copies of each image (up to 18

  copies of one name in Suspicious Activity).
  Fix: Keep one copy per original (seed 42).
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 4
  Problem: Synthetic smoke in cctv security: about 2,390 images are smoke pasted

  onto street or room photos (H_*, M_*, FireData*).
  Fix: Exclude them. The 6k set has 12,959 real smoke boxes.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 5
  Problem: Leakage: all 25 theft clips and 771 Suspicious Activity groups sit in

  more than one split. 526 images are shared between 6k and Fire-Smoke-CCTV,
  517 between Suspicious Activity and cctv, and one gun video has 5,124 frames.
  Fix: Ignore the old splits. Re-split group-aware, with frame caps per clip.

  Two smaller issues:
  - The 4,760 normal_* images in the 6k set are meant as "no fire" background,
    but some contain people, and there are far too many of them. Use them only
    after auto-labelling, capped at 5–10% of train.
  - 46 6k images whose names start with fire_ or smoke_ have empty labels.
    They'll go on a review list.

  Decisions needed (recommended answer in bold)

  1. Knives and swords → weapon? Drop for now, and leave out images that only
     contain blades.
  2. Smoking (cigarettes) → drop, and leave out images that only contain it?
     Yes.
  3. Theft mask → drop? Yes.
  4. Fire and smoke → one fire_smoke class? Yes.
  5. Behaviour boxes:
     - theft and the 6 whole-body Surveillance classes → person. Yes.
     - hiding_face1 → drop. Yes.
     - stealing → drop. Yes.
  6. Three-wheelers → vehicle, bicycles → drop? Yes.
  7. Auto pre-label missing people and vehicles, then you review? Yes.
  8. Was removing Automotive intentional? It had 22,433 vehicle boxes. Recommend
     putting it (or another vehicle-labelled set) back. Without it, vehicle
     depends entirely on unreviewed auto labels.
  9. Keep one copy per original; at most 150 frames per video clip; no class
     more than 5× the smallest? Yes.
  10. Exclude the synthetic cctv smoke and the off-topic images you confirm? Put
      the 46 empty-label 6k fire/smoke images on the review list? Yes.
  11. None of the 9 folder names follows the naming pattern. Use short source
      ids such as fire__rf_fire-smoke-6k and weapon__rf_cctv-security? Yes.
  12. Keep fire-dataset even though its licence is unknown? Yes, for research.
  13. Use the 6k "normal" images and the empty Fire-Smoke-CCTV frames as
      background, after auto-labelling and capped at 5–10% of train? Yes.
  14. Add your own photos of service-centre bays? Yes.

  Housekeeping

  - The draft class map now uses the 9 current folder names. I regenerated all
    reports/01_* files, configs/class_map.draft.yaml and docs/sources.md.
  - I viewed sample images only for the two sources that are new or changed (6k
    and cctv v5), 10 each.
  - The vehicle numbers and percentages are estimates from 30-image samples.

  If data/raw/ changes again, tell me once the folder is final, so the decisions
  and the Phase 3 build are based on the right set of sources.

       71  ## 4. Class counts after the proposed mapping vs the target (≥

⏺ Phase 1 audit (fourth run, 11 sources): not ready to train

  Automotive and Knife Detection are back in data/raw/, so I re-audited all 11
  sources (63,122 images, about 31,100 originals once augmented copies are
  removed). Both returning folders gave exactly the same numbers as on
  2026-10-09, so they're the same exports. Nothing in data/raw/ was changed. The
  full report is in 01_audit_report.md (reports/01_audit_report.md).

  Can I train now? No.

  Every class now clears the 200-image target, and all labels are technically
  valid (0 malformed lines). The data still can't be trained on as it is: people
  and vehicles are left unboxed in most sources, there are augmented copies,
  the old splits leak, about 2,390 images are synthetic smoke, and the classes
  are imbalanced. After Phase 3, I expect Yes, with warnings, because the auto
  labels won't have been reviewed yet.

  ┌───────────────────────┬───────────┬─────────────────────────────────────┐
  │         Class         │ Originals │                Note                 │
  ├───────────────────────┼───────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ person                │ 4,846     │                                     │
  ├───────────────────────┼───────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ vehicle               │ 1,472     │ all from 12 Automotive videos       │
  ├───────────────────────┼───────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ weapon (guns and      │ 7,832     │                                     │
  │ rifles)               │           │                                     │
  ├───────────────────────┼───────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ fire_smoke            │ 9,569     │ 6.5× vehicle, above the 5× warning  │
  │                       │           │ level                               │
  └───────────────────────┴───────────┴─────────────────────────────────────┘

  Top 5 problems and fixes

  #: 1
  Problem: People and vehicles left unboxed. Vehicles are missing boxes in
  10–83%
  of images in 9 of 11 sources. People are missing boxes in 20–100% of images
  in 9 of 11 sources.
  Fix: Auto-label with yolo11s, mark the boxes auto, then you review. Estimate:
  +2,000–3,500 vehicle images.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 2
  Problem: Augmented copies: 3× in 5 sources, plus hidden 5× copies in
  Automotive's train split, even though its README says "no augmentation".
  Fix: Keep one copy per original (seed 42).
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 3
  Problem: Leakage: all 25 theft clips, 6 of the 12 Automotive videos and 768
  Suspicious Activity groups sit in more than one split. 3,663 Knife Detection
  images are near-duplicates of Suspicious Activity, and one gun video has
  5,124 frames.
  Fix: Ignore the old splits. Re-split group-aware, cutting long videos into
  blocks of consecutive frames and capping frames per clip.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 4
  Problem: Synthetic smoke in cctv: about 2,390 images have smoke pasted onto
  streets or rooms (H_*, M_*, FireData*).
  Fix: Exclude them. The 6k set has 12,959 real smoke boxes.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 5
  Problem: Imbalance and narrow vehicle data: fire_smoke has 6.5× as many
  originals as vehicle. All vehicle boxes come from 12 phone videos of moving
  traffic, with no parked cars.
  Fix: Cap fire_smoke and weapon at 5× the smallest class, add auto-labelled
  vehicles from the fire and theft scenes, and add your own bay photos.

  Decisions needed (recommended answer in bold)

  1. Knives and swords → weapon? Drop for now, and leave out images that only
     contain blades. Including them would add about 6,200 weapon originals and
     make the imbalance worse.
  2. Smoking (cigarettes) → drop, and leave out images that only contain it?
     Yes.
  3. Theft mask → drop? Yes.
  4. Fire and smoke → one fire_smoke class? Yes.
  5. Behaviour boxes:
     - theft and the 6 whole-body Surveillance classes → person. Yes.
     - hiding_face1 → drop. Yes.
     - stealing → drop. Yes.
  6. Vehicle types:
     - Automotive car, bus and motorcycle → vehicle. Yes.
     - other-vehicle → vehicle. Yes. It also includes cycle-rickshaws and carts.
     - Three-wheelers → vehicle. Yes. The pretrained model can't detect them, so
       they'd need hand labels.
     - Bicycles → drop. Yes.
  7. Auto pre-label missing people and vehicles, then you review? Yes.
  8. Keep data/raw/ fixed from now on? It changed twice during the audit. Yes,
     so that decisions and the build match this report.
  9. Keep one copy per original, at most 150 frames per video clip, and no class
     more than 5× the smallest? Yes.
  10. Exclude the synthetic cctv smoke and the off-topic images you confirm? Put
      the 46 empty-label 6k fire/smoke images on the review list? Yes.
  11. Use short source ids in file names (for example vehicle__rf_automotive and
      fire__rf_fire-smoke-6k)? Yes. None of the 11 folder names follows the
      naming pattern.
  12. Keep fire-dataset even though its licence is unknown? Yes, for research.
  13. Use the 6k "normal" images and the empty Fire-Smoke-CCTV frames as
      background, after auto-labelling and capped at 5–10% of train? Yes.
  14. Add your own photos of service-centre bays? Yes. They'd be kept private.

  Housekeeping

  - I regenerated all Phase 1 outputs for the 11 sources.
  - The draft class map now includes Automotive and Knife Detection again.
  - I didn't need to view any new sample images; the content checks reuse the
    earlier viewings.
  - The vehicle estimates and percentages come from 30-image samples.
  - docs/decision_log.md stays empty until you answer.

⏺ I'll add the privacy note (readable licence plates) to the parking source's
  entry in docs/sources.md, then do the final check that data/raw is untouched.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Phase 1 audit (fifth run, 11 sources): not ready to train

  data/raw/ changed again: Automotive was removed and
  Vehicle-detection-parking.v1i.yolov11 was added. I re-audited all 11 sources
  (68,868 images, about 40,400 originals once augmented copies are removed).
  Nothing in data/raw/ was changed. The full report is in 01_audit_report.md
  (reports/01_audit_report.md).

  Can I train now? No.

  The new parking set is the best vehicle data so far: real CCTV at a car-park
  entrance, with 34,257 well-drawn vehicle boxes. All four classes clear the
  200-image target, and the class balance is fine: the largest class has 2.9× as
  many images as the smallest, under the 5× limit. The data still can't be
  trained on as it is: people are left unboxed almost everywhere, there are
  augmented copies, the old splits leak, and about 2,390 images are synthetic
  smoke. After Phase 3, I expect Yes, with warnings, because the auto labels
  won't have been reviewed yet.

  ┌─────────────────────┬───────────┬───────────────────────────────────────┐
  │        Class        │ Originals │              Main source              │
  ├─────────────────────┼───────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ person              │ 3,663     │ cctv only                             │
  ├─────────────────────┼───────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ vehicle             │ 10,778    │ parking only                          │
  ├─────────────────────┼───────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ weapon (guns and    │ 7,832     │ Knife Detection, Suspicious Activity, │
  │ rifles)             │           │  cctv                                 │
  ├─────────────────────┼───────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ fire_smoke          │ 9,569     │ 6k, Car Fires, Fire-Smoke-CCTV, cctv, │
  │                     │           │  fire-dataset                         │
  └─────────────────────┴───────────┴───────────────────────────────────────┘

  Top 5 problems and fixes

  #: 1
  Problem: People and vehicles left unboxed. People: 20–100% of images in 9 of
  11
  sources, including parking (23%: guards and riders). Vehicles: 10–83% in the
  fire and theft sources, and 40% in parking, mostly cars cut off at the image
  edge (an estimate).
  Fix: Auto-label with yolo11s, adding only boxes that don't overlap existing
  ones. Mark them auto, then you review.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 2
  Problem: Augmented copies: 5 sources contain 3× copies of each image.
  Fix: Keep one copy per original (seed 42).
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 3
  Problem: Leakage: all 4 parking cameras and all 25 theft clips sit in more
  than
  one split. 3,663 Knife Detection images are near-duplicates of Suspicious
  Activity, and one gun video has 5,124 frames.
  Fix: Re-split group-aware: parking by camera plus 30-minute block, long videos

  in frame blocks with caps.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 4
  Problem: Synthetic smoke in cctv: about 2,390 images (H_*, M_*, FireData*).
  Fix: Exclude them. The 6k set has 12,959 real smoke boxes.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 5
  Problem: Narrow and private vehicle data: 4 cameras at one car park over 2
  days, no three-wheelers. Licence plates are readable, and the licence is
  "Private".
  Fix: Keep it but treat it as private. Add auto-labelled vehicles from other
  scenes and your own bay photos.

  Decisions needed (recommended answer in bold)

  1. Knives and swords → weapon? Drop for now, and leave out images that only
     contain blades.
  2. Smoking (cigarettes) → drop, and leave out images that only contain it?
     Yes.
  3. Theft mask → drop? Yes.
  4. Fire and smoke → one fire_smoke class? Yes.
  5. Behaviour boxes:
     - theft and the 6 whole-body Surveillance classes → person. Yes.
     - hiding_face1 → drop. Yes.

  Decisions needed:
  1. face_covered box style is mixed. hiding_face1 and Theft mask box the face
     or head, while hiding_face2 boxes the whole body, so the model gets mixed
     signals about what to box. My recommendation: keep it as is for this
     version and judge from the validation results. The alternative is to re-box
     hiding_face2 around the head by hand (617 boxes).
  2. Imbalance is now about 19× (vehicle 10,778 vs loitering 557). Capping
     everything at 5× the smallest would cut each big class to about 2,800
     originals. My recommendation: cap the big classes at about 3,000 originals
     each, accept roughly 5× for loitering, and plan more loitering data (your
     own staged clips).

  │ tampering    │ 1,027                                            │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ loitering    │ 551                                              │
  └──────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Top 5 problems and fixes

  #: 1
  Problem: People and vehicles left unboxed. Surveillance, theft and vehicle
  theft have 0 person boxes. Elsewhere, people are unboxed in 20–100% of
  sampled images and vehicles in up to 83%.
  Fix: Auto-label people and vehicles, mark them auto, then you review.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 2
  Problem: About 20× imbalance, and little variety in the behaviour classes.
  Loitering comes from about 20 phone clips; tampering from 134 groups.
  Fix: Cap the big classes at about 3,000 each, and add your own staged clips.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 3
  Problem: Copies and leakage in the uncleaned sources. 6k, Car Fires, vehicle
  theft and Suspicious Activity have 3× copies. The theft clips and all 4
  parking cameras sit in more than one split, and 3,663 Knife Detection images
  are near-duplicates of Suspicious Activity.
  Fix: One copy per original everywhere, then a group-aware re-split.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 4
  Problem: Knife labels are inconsistent. Suspicious Activity knives count as
  weapons, but Knife Detection knife and cctv knife/Sword are still undecided.
  3,198 Knife Detection images contain only blades.
  Fix: Decision 1.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 5
  Problem: Behaviour from a single frame. face_covered mixes face-sized and
  whole-body boxes, and loitering depends on time, which one frame can't show.
  Fix: Decision 2. Keep the tracking-time rule for loitering.

  Decisions needed (recommended answer in bold)

  1. Knife Detection knife and cctv knife/Sword → weapon? Yes, all of them, for
     consistency with Suspicious Activity.
  2. face_covered box style? Keep the boxes as they are for now. Re-box
     hiding_face2 around the head only if the class scores poorly.
  3. Caps? Cap vehicle, weapon and fire_smoke at about 3,000 originals each, and
     accept about 5× for loitering.
  4. One copy per original and 150 frames per clip for the remaining sources,
     with parking grouped by camera plus 30-minute block? Yes.
  5. Exclude off-topic images, and send the 46 empty-label 6k fire/smoke images
     and the 130 FireData smoke images to review? Yes.
  6. Bicycles → drop, three-wheelers → vehicle (hand labels needed)? Yes.
  7. Short source ids in file names? Yes.
  8. Keep the parking set ("Private" licence, readable plates) and fire-dataset
     (unknown licence) for research, treating the parking images as private?
     Yes.
  9. Use the 6k "normal" frames as background, after auto-labelling and capped
     at 5–10% of train? Yes. They're about 6% of the data now.
  10. Add your own staged photos and clips? Yes.
  11. Fire and smoke → one fire_smoke class? Yes.
  12. Auto pre-label people and vehicles? Yes. Every person needs a person box.
  13. Freeze data/raw/ with these 11 folders? Yes.
  14. Update CLAUDE.md to the 7-class contract? Yes, if you confirm. I'm not
      allowed to edit that file without your permission.

  The "after decided fixes" counts are estimates, and the unboxed percentages
  come from 30-image samples. The six decisions already made are listed at the
  top of section 6 of the report.

⏺ Written for: you and any teammates who help check or draw boxes. The full
  guide is in docs/labelling_guide.md.

  Datasets with missing boxes

  Every dataset is missing some boxes. In total, about 17,000 of the ~37,000
  images we're keeping (≈46%) need boxes added. That figure is an estimate from
  YOLO checks on 30 random images per dataset.

  ┌───────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┬──────────────┐
  │          Dataset          │           Missing            │  Images to   │
  │                           │                              │  fix (est.)  │
  ├───────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼──────────────┤
  │ Vehicle-detection-parking │ people (guards, riders);     │ ~6,470       │
  │                           │ cars cut off at the edge     │              │
  ├───────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼──────────────┤
  │ Knife Detection           │ people                       │ ~2,970       │
  ├───────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼──────────────┤
  │ Fire And Smoke 6k         │ people, vehicles             │ ~2,180       │
  ├───────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼──────────────┤
  │ Surveillance              │ people in every image (0     │ ~2,140       │
  │                           │ person boxes); vehicles      │              │
  ├───────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼──────────────┤
  │ Suspicious Activity       │ people                       │ ~790         │
  ├───────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼──────────────┤
  │ cctv security (cleaned)   │ vehicles, a few people       │ ~770         │
  ├───────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼──────────────┤
  │ Car Fires                 │ vehicles (often the burning  │ ~630         │
  │                           │ car itself), people          │              │
  ├───────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼──────────────┤
  │ Fire-Smoke-CCTV (cleaned) │ people, vehicles             │ ~410         │
  ├───────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼──────────────┤
  │                           │ people, vehicles,            │              │
  │ theft                     │ face_covered (masked         │ all 275      │
  │                           │ thieves)                     │              │
  ├───────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼──────────────┤
  │ fire-dataset              │ vehicles, people             │ ~250         │
  ├───────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┼──────────────┤
  │ vehicle theft detection   │ vehicles, people (dark night │ ~150+        │
  │                           │  images), face_covered       │              │
  └───────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────┴──────────────┘

  No dataset labels three-wheelers.

  How it gets fixed

  1. Auto pre-labelling (script): the pretrained YOLO11s model adds person and
     vehicle boxes where they're missing. These are the only two of our classes
     it can detect. Each image it touches is marked auto.
  2. Review (you): in a free labelling tool that runs on your own computer
     (X-AnyLabeling or Label Studio), fix wrong boxes and add missed ones. Don't
     use online tools: the project rule is no uploads, and the parking images
     show number plates.
  3. Hand labelling (you): add what the model can't detect: face_covered in the
     theft datasets, three-wheelers, and any weapons or fire the datasets
     missed.
  4. Validate and rebuild (script, Phase 5).

  Step by step

  1. Answer the open boxing decisions (below), or follow each one's "until
     decided" rule.
  2. I build review packages in data/review/to_label/<source>/, with the auto
     boxes already drawn.
  3. Install the tool and set the 7 classes in exact ID order.
  4. Review in this priority order:
     a. Test-split images that have auto boxes.
     b. theft, vehicle theft and Surveillance.
     c. 50 auto-labelled images per dataset. If more than 5 have an error,
        review that whole dataset.
  5. Hand-label the missing classes.
  6. Export as YOLO into data/manual_labels/ with the file names unchanged. An
     image with nothing to box gets an empty .txt.
  7. Ask me to run Phase 5.

  Main boxing rules (all in the guide)

  - Coverage: box every visible instance of all 7 classes, including small or
    partly hidden ones.
  - Tightness: boxes are tight around the visible part only. Don't guess hidden
    parts, and don't cut anything off.
  - Never box: posters, screens, reflections, toys or watermarks.
  - person: head to feet. A rider gets a person box plus a vehicle box for the
    motorcycle.
  - vehicle: the whole vehicle, including mirrors and open doors. A burning car
    gets both vehicle and fire_smoke. Bicycles get no box.
  - weapon: the weapon only, not the hand. Tools like crowbars and screwdrivers
    aren't weapons.
  - fire_smoke:
    - One box per separate fire.
    - Smoke means the clearly visible plume, not thin haze.
    - Exhaust, fog, steam and headlights aren't fire_smoke.
  - Behaviours: the person keeps their person box, and the behaviour box is
    added on top:
    - tampering and loitering: box the whole person.
    - loitering: label it only when the clip context shows it.
    - face_covered: box the head.

  Open decisions that change the boxing

  1. Do swords count as weapon?
  2. face_covered: head box or whole-body box?
  3. stealing boxes: re-box them to the person only?
  4. Do motorcycle full-face helmets count as face_covered?
  5. Three-wheelers → vehicle?
  6. Small flames (candles, lighters) → no fire_smoke box?

  Each has an "until decided" rule in the guide, so labelling can start now.

  The per-image time saving in the guide (5–10 s to correct a pre-drawn image vs
  30–60 s to draw from scratch) is my guess. When you're ready, I can build the
  review packages (Phase 3 steps 1–4).

❯ What is the most suitable tool for auto boundary box and labelling tasks?
  Don’t think about vehicle number plates; then suggest the most suitable
  online tool for adding boundary boxes and labelling automatically in the
  minimum time. I want to add boxes to all of the missing boxes in the dataset.
  Then suggest the most suitable one. If I use Google Colab, can you give
  guidance on adding boundary boxes(annotations) and labelling missing boxes
  and labels in existing datasets? And for the spatial thing, can you give
  Python code in YOLO version 11 format for google colabe annotation and
  labelling all of the datasets? Do not take action; I just want your opinions.

⏺ You asked for opinions only, so I haven't run anything or changed any files.

  Short answer

  - Best tool for adding the missing boxes automatically: a Google Colab
    notebook running Ultralytics YOLO11x, with the script below. It's free, uses
    a GPU, writes YOLO11 format directly, handles all 11 datasets in one run,
    and keeps your existing labels. A rough guess is 20–40 minutes on a free T4
    for all ~69,000 images.
  - Best online tool for checking and correcting the boxes:
    - Fastest: Roboflow Annotate. It imports YOLO folders by drag and drop, has
      model-assisted labelling and quick keyboard review, and exports YOLOv11.
      The catch: on the free plan, projects are public (and the parking set's
      licence is "Private").
    - Fast and private: CVAT online (cvat.ai). It imports and exports YOLO too
      and has AI-assisted tools. The free plan has limits on projects and
      storage, so check the current limits.
    - My pick is Colab for the auto-boxing and CVAT for the review. Choose
      Roboflow only if publishing the images is acceptable to you.
  - What no tool can box automatically and reliably: tampering, loitering,
    face_covered and three-wheelers. A COCO-trained model only knows person and
    vehicles. Open-vocabulary models (YOLO-World, Grounding DINO) can suggest
    things like "balaclava", but with low precision. Those classes need a person
    to label them.

  Tool comparison

  Tool: Colab + YOLO11x script
  Auto-boxing: Best for person/vehicle; whole dataset in one run
  Review speed: none (no editor)
  Cost / privacy: free; files go to your own Drive
  Verdict: Use this for the auto boxes
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Tool: Roboflow Annotate
  Auto-boxing: Label Assist + Auto Label from text prompts (uses credits)
  Review speed: fastest
  Cost / privacy: free plan = public project
  Verdict: Fastest review, but public
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Tool: CVAT online
  Auto-boxing: AI tools (detector, SAM) per task
  Review speed: fast
  Cost / privacy: free tier, private
  Verdict: Use this for the review
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Tool: Label Studio
  Auto-boxing: needs an ML backend set up
  Review speed: medium
  Cost / privacy: free, local or self-hosted
  Verdict: OK, more setup
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Tool: X-AnyLabeling
  Auto-boxing: built-in YOLO11 / SAM / Grounding DINO
  Review speed: fast
  Cost / privacy: free, offline desktop
  Verdict: Best if you change your mind about online tools

  Project rules: CLAUDE.md rule 5 says never upload or share images. Colab is
  already part of the plan (training), but reviewing in an online tool is a
  change to that rule. If you go ahead, I'll log it as your decision.

  Colab workflow

  1. On your computer, zip one folder per dataset into sources.zip:
     - the 9 raw folders;
     - the cleaned copies of cctv security and Fire-Smoke-CCTV from
       data/interim/.
  2. Upload sources.zip to Google Drive (MyDrive/component4/). In Colab, choose
     Runtime → Change runtime type → GPU.
  3. Run the cells below. The script:
     - converts every existing label to the 7 target IDs and drops the dropped
       classes;
     - adds person and vehicle boxes only where none exists (it skips an auto
       box that overlaps an existing box of the same class by IoU > 0.5);
     - keeps your behaviour boxes, so a person gets both boxes;
     - logs every auto box to auto_boxes.csv.
  4. Check the visual sample (cell 6), then zip the output back to Drive.
  5. Import each dataset folder into CVAT or Roboflow (YOLO / Ultralytics YOLO
     format with data.yaml) and review in the order from the labelling guide.
     When you export the corrected labels, they go into data/manual_labels/.
     Unreviewed output goes into data/interim/ and counts as auto.

  Label boxes on all copies if you like; GPU time is cheap. But review only the
  kept originals (~37,000), not all 69,000 images, because augmented copies get
  removed in Phase 3 anyway.

  Code (Colab, YOLO11 format)

  # ── Cell 1: install (same version as the local .venv)
  ─────────────────────────
  !pip -q install ultralytics==8.4.174

  # ── Cell 2: data
  ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  from google.colab import drive
  drive.mount('/content/drive')
  !mkdir -p /content/sources && unzip -qo
  "/content/drive/MyDrive/component4/sources.zip" -d /content/sources
  !ls /content/sources

  # ── Cell 3: settings
  ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  from pathlib import Path

  SRC_ROOT = Path('/content/sources')      # one folder per dataset
  OUT_ROOT = Path('/content/autolabel')    # new folder; the originals are never
  overwritten
  MODEL = 'yolo11x.pt'                     # most accurate YOLO11 (COCO); use
  yolo11l.pt if GPU memory is short
  CONF, IMGSZ, BATCH = 0.40, 640, 32
  SKIP_IOU = 0.5     # skip an auto box that overlaps an existing same-class box
  this much
  DEDUP_IOU = 0.7    # car + truck detected on the same object -> keep one
  vehicle box
  MIN_AREA = 0.0005  # ignore auto boxes smaller than 0.05% of the image
  COPY_IMAGES = True # copy images next to the labels (needed for review tools)

  TARGET = ['person', 'vehicle', 'weapon', 'fire_smoke', 'tampering',
  'loitering', 'face_covered']
  COCO_TO_TARGET = {0: 'person', 2: 'vehicle', 3: 'vehicle', 5: 'vehicle', 7:
  'vehicle'}  # bicycle (1) not used

  FIRE = {'fire': 'fire_smoke', 'smoke': 'fire_smoke'}
  AUTO = ['person', 'vehicle']
  # source class name -> target class (None = drop). A class missing from the
  map is reported, never guessed.
  SOURCES = {
      'Vehicle-detection-parking.v1i.yolov11': dict(map={'car': 'vehicle',
  'bus': 'vehicle', 'truck': 'vehicle',
                                                         'motorcycle':
  'vehicle', 'bicycle': None}, auto=AUTO),
      'Fire And Smoke Detection.v1-fire_smoke_detection_6k.yolov11':
  dict(map=FIRE, auto=AUTO),
      'Fire-Smoke-CCTV.v6i.yolov11': dict(map=FIRE, auto=AUTO),            #
  cleaned copy
      'Car Fires Detection.v2i.yolov11': dict(map={'fire': 'fire_smoke'},
  auto=AUTO),
      'fire-dataset': dict(map={'fire': 'fire_smoke'}, auto=AUTO),          #
  Pascal VOC xml
      'Knife Detection.v2i.yolov11': dict(map={'gun': 'weapon', 'rifle':
  'weapon',
                                               'knife': 'weapon'}, auto=AUTO),
  # knife: open decision 1
      'Suspicious Activity.v2i.yolov11': dict(map={'Gun': 'weapon', 'Knife':
  'weapon', 'Smoking': None}, auto=AUTO),
      'cctv security.v5i.yolov11': dict(map={'Person': 'person', 'Gun':
  'weapon', 'knife': 'weapon',
                                             'Sword': 'weapon',
  # Sword: open decision 1
                                             'Fire': 'fire_smoke', 'Smoke':
  'fire_smoke',
                                             'Theft mask': 'face_covered'},
  auto=AUTO),  # cleaned copy
      'Surveillance.v1i.yolov11': dict(map={'Loitering': 'loitering', 'Spying':
  'loitering',
                                            'Tampering': 'tampering',
  'breaking_in': 'tampering',
                                            'scalling': 'tampering',
  'hiding_face1': 'face_covered',
                                            'hiding_face2': 'face_covered'},
  auto=AUTO),
      'theft.v1i.yolov11': dict(map={'theft': 'tampering'}, auto=AUTO),
      'vehicle theft detection.v2i.yolov11': dict(map={'stealing': 'tampering'},
  auto=AUTO),
  }

  # ── Cell 4: helpers
  ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  import csv, random, shutil, xml.etree.ElementTree as ET
  from collections import Counter
  import yaml

  IMG_EXT = {'.jpg', '.jpeg', '.png', '.bmp', '.webp'}
  SPLIT = {'train': 'train', 'valid': 'valid', 'val': 'valid', 'validation':
  'valid', 'test': 'test'}

  def class_names(src):
      f = src / 'data.yaml'
      if not f.exists():
          return None                                    # VOC source: names
  come from the xml files
      n = yaml.safe_load(f.read_text())['names']
      return [n[k] for k in sorted(n)] if isinstance(n, dict) else list(n)

  def label_file(img):
      for sub, ext in (('labels', '.txt'), ('annotations', '.xml')):
          p = img.parent.parent / sub / (img.stem + ext)
          if p.exists():
              return p
      return None

  def read_boxes(lab, names, W, H):
      """Existing boxes as (source class name, cx, cy, w, h), normalised 0-1.
  Polygons become boxes."""
      if lab is None:
          return []
      out = []
      if lab.suffix == '.xml':
          for o in ET.parse(lab).getroot().iter('object'):
              b = o.find('bndbox')
              x0, y0, x1, y1 = (float(b.findtext(k)) for k in ('xmin', 'ymin',
  'xmax', 'ymax'))
              out.append((o.findtext('name').strip(), (x0 + x1) / 2 / W, (y0 +
  y1) / 2 / H, (x1 - x0) / W, (y1 - y0) / H))
          return out
      for line in lab.read_text().splitlines():
          v = line.split()
          if len(v) < 5:
              continue
          c, xy = int(float(v[0])), [float(x) for x in v[1:]]
          if len(xy) == 4:
              cx, cy, w, h = xy
          else:                                          # segmentation polygon
  -> min/max box
              xs, ys = xy[0::2], xy[1::2]
              cx, cy, w, h = (min(xs) + max(xs)) / 2, (min(ys) + max(ys)) / 2,
  max(xs) - min(xs), max(ys) - min(ys)
          out.append((names[c] if names and c < len(names) else str(c), cx, cy,
  w, h))
      return out

  def iou(a, b):
      ax0, ay0, ax1, ay1 = a[0] - a[2] / 2, a[1] - a[3] / 2, a[0] + a[2] / 2,
  a[1] + a[3] / 2
      bx0, by0, bx1, by1 = b[0] - b[2] / 2, b[1] - b[3] / 2, b[0] + b[2] / 2,
  b[1] + b[3] / 2
      inter = max(0, min(ax1, bx1) - max(ax0, bx0)) * max(0, min(ay1, by1) -
  max(ay0, by0))
      union = a[2] * a[3] + b[2] * b[3] - inter
      return inter / union if union > 0 else 0.0

  def clip_box(cx, cy, w, h):
      x0, y0, x1, y1 = max(0, cx - w / 2), max(0, cy - h / 2), min(1, cx + w /
  2), min(1, cy + h / 2)
      return (x0 + x1) / 2, (y0 + y1) / 2, x1 - x0, y1 - y0

  # ── Cell 5: auto-label all datasets
  ────────────────────────────────────────────
  from ultralytics import YOLO

  model = YOLO(MODEL)
  shutil.rmtree(OUT_ROOT, ignore_errors=True)
  log_rows = []

  for sid, cfg in SOURCES.items():
      src = SRC_ROOT / sid
      if not src.exists():
          print('NOT FOUND, skipped:', sid)
          continue
      names = class_names(src)
      imgs = sorted(p for p in src.rglob('*') if p.suffix.lower() in IMG_EXT and
  p.parent.name == 'images')
      unknown, added, changed = Counter(), Counter(), 0
      for i in range(0, len(imgs), BATCH):
          chunk = imgs[i:i + BATCH]
          results = model.predict([str(p) for p in chunk], conf=CONF,
  imgsz=IMGSZ, device=0, half=True,
                                  batch=BATCH, classes=list(COCO_TO_TARGET),
  verbose=False)
          for img, r in zip(chunk, results):
              H, W = r.orig_shape
              existing = []                              # dataset boxes,
  remapped, original order kept
              for name, *b in read_boxes(label_file(img), names, W, H):
                  if name not in cfg['map']:
                      unknown[name] += 1
                      continue
                  if cfg['map'][name]:
                      existing.append((TARGET.index(cfg['map'][name]),
  *clip_box(*b)))
              autos = []
              dets = sorted(zip(r.boxes.conf.tolist(), r.boxes.cls.tolist(),
  r.boxes.xywhn.tolist()), reverse=True)
              for cf, c, b in dets:
                  t, b = COCO_TO_TARGET[int(c)], clip_box(*b)
                  if t not in cfg['auto'] or b[2] * b[3] < MIN_AREA:
                      continue
                  tid = TARGET.index(t)
                  if any(e[0] == tid and iou(e[1:], b) > SKIP_IOU for e in
  existing):
                      continue                           # already boxed in the
  dataset
                  if any(a[0] == tid and iou(a[1:5], b) > DEDUP_IOU for a in
  autos):
                      continue                           # duplicate detection
  of the same object
                  autos.append((tid, *b, cf))
              split = SPLIT.get(img.relative_to(src).parts[0].lower(), 'train')
              d = OUT_ROOT / sid / split
              (d / 'labels').mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
              lines = [f'{c} {x:.6f} {y:.6f} {w:.6f} {h:.6f}' for c, x, y, w, h
  in existing]
              lines += [f'{a[0]} {a[1]:.6f} {a[2]:.6f} {a[3]:.6f} {a[4]:.6f}'
  for a in autos]
              (d / 'labels' / f'{img.stem}.txt').write_text('\n'.join(lines) +
  ('\n' if lines else ''))
              if COPY_IMAGES:
                  (d / 'images').mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
                  shutil.copy2(img, d / 'images' / img.name)
              for a in autos:
                  added[TARGET[a[0]]] += 1
                  log_rows.append([sid, split, img.name, TARGET[a[0]],
  f'{a[5]:.3f}', *(f'{v:.6f}' for v in a[1:5])])
              changed += bool(autos)
      (OUT_ROOT / sid / 'data.yaml').write_text(yaml.safe_dump(
          {'path': '.', 'train': 'train/images', 'val': 'valid/images', 'test':
  'test/images',
           'names': dict(enumerate(TARGET))}, sort_keys=False))
      print(f'{sid}: {len(imgs)} images, {changed} got auto boxes {dict(added)}'
            + (f'  UNMAPPED (not written): {dict(unknown)}' if unknown else ''))

  with open(OUT_ROOT / 'auto_boxes.csv', 'w', newline='') as f:
      w = csv.writer(f)
      w.writerow(['source', 'split', 'image', 'class', 'conf', 'cx', 'cy', 'w',
  'h'])
      w.writerows(log_rows)
  print('auto boxes total:', len(log_rows))

  # ── Cell 6: visual check (blue = dataset box, red = auto box)
  ──────────────────
  import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
  from PIL import Image, ImageDraw

  auto = {}
  for s, sp, name, cls, cf, *b in log_rows:
      auto.setdefault((s, sp, name), []).append(tuple(map(float, b)))
  random.seed(42)
  pick = random.sample(sorted(auto), min(12, len(auto)))
  fig, axs = plt.subplots(3, 4, figsize=(20, 15))
  for ax, (s, sp, name) in zip(axs.flat, pick):
      im = Image.open(OUT_ROOT / s / sp / 'images' / name).convert('RGB')
      W, H = im.size
      dr = ImageDraw.Draw(im)
      for line in (OUT_ROOT / s / sp / 'labels' / (Path(name).stem +
  '.txt')).read_text().splitlines():
          c, x, y, w, h = line.split()
          x, y, w, h = map(float, (x, y, w, h))
          is_auto = any(abs(x - a[0]) < 1e-5 and abs(y - a[1]) < 1e-5 for a in
  auto[(s, sp, name)])
          dr.rectangle([(x - w / 2) * W, (y - h / 2) * H, (x + w / 2) * W, (y +
  h / 2) * H],
                       outline='red' if is_auto else 'blue', width=3)
          dr.text(((x - w / 2) * W + 3, (y - h / 2) * H + 3), TARGET[int(c)],
  fill='yellow')
      ax.imshow(im); ax.set_title(f'{s[:28]} | {name[:24]}', fontsize=8);
  ax.axis('off')
  plt.tight_layout(); plt.show()

  # ── Cell 7: save to Drive
  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  !cd /content && zip -qr autolabel.zip autolabel && cp autolabel.zip
  /content/drive/MyDrive/component4/

  # ── Cell 8 (optional): hints for face_covered — NOT merged, review only
  ────────
  # YOLO-World is open-vocabulary (YOLOv8-based; there is no YOLO11 "World"
  model). Precision is low,
  # so its boxes go to a separate folder and are only used as hints while
  reviewing.
  from ultralytics import YOLOWorld

  HINTS = {'theft.v1i.yolov11': ['balaclava', 'ski mask', 'face mask'],
           'vehicle theft detection.v2i.yolov11': ['balaclava', 'ski mask',
  'face mask']}
  world = YOLOWorld('yolov8x-worldv2.pt')
  fc = TARGET.index('face_covered')
  for sid, prompts in HINTS.items():
      world.set_classes(prompts)
      for img in sorted(p for p in (SRC_ROOT / sid).rglob('*') if
  p.suffix.lower() in IMG_EXT and p.parent.name == 'images'):
          r = world.predict(str(img), conf=0.15, device=0, verbose=False)[0]
          split = SPLIT.get(img.relative_to(SRC_ROOT / sid).parts[0].lower(),
  'train')
          d = OUT_ROOT / '_hints_face_covered' / sid / split / 'labels'
          d.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
          lines = [f'{fc} ' + ' '.join(f'{v:.6f}' for v in clip_box(*b)) for b
  in r.boxes.xywhn.tolist()]
          (d / f'{img.stem}.txt').write_text('\n'.join(lines) + ('\n' if lines
  else ''))

  Notes on the code

  - Box order: the dataset's own boxes come first, in their original order, and
    auto boxes are appended. Original files are never overwritten.
  - Unknown classes: a class missing from the map is reported and not written,
    so a typo can't silently change a class.
  - Knives and swords: the map follows my recommendation for decision 1 (knives
    and swords → weapon). Change those two lines if you decide otherwise.
  - Behaviour images and background: Smoking-only images and the frame caps are
    not applied here; that's the Phase 3 build. Images with nothing in them get
    an empty .txt.
  - Expected mistakes: auto boxes will include people on posters and screens,
    and will miss some night-IR people. The red boxes in cell 6 show what to
    check. Three-wheelers are often detected as car, motorcycle or truck, so
    they become vehicle automatically. Those are useful, but still check them.
  - Speed: the 20–40 minute run time is a guess for the free T4. Free Colab
    sessions can disconnect, so save the output (cell 7) as soon as the run
    finishes.

❯ I want to check and add boundary boxes and label missing data to all datasets
  in 11 folders. Is that okay, and when we add the zip file to Google Drive,
  should all 11 folders be compressed into one zip file or maintain separate
  zip files for all 11 folders? And before training the dataset, I want to
  remove duplicate images from the 69,000 images; can the duplicate image
  removal process be done in Colab or not? I do not want your action or coding
  support at this moment; I just want your suggestions only.

⏺ These are suggestions only. I haven't run or changed anything.

  1. Checking and adding boxes in all 11 folders

  Yes, that's the right goal. Every image needs a box for every visible person,
  vehicle, weapon, fire and behaviour. A few points will save you a lot of time:

  - Remove duplicates first, then label. If you label all 69,000 images, you'll
    also label the copies that get thrown away later. After duplicate removal,
    about 37,000 images remain (my estimate), so you'd save roughly 45% of the
    work.
  - Use the cleaned copies of cctv security and Fire-Smoke-CCTV from
    data/interim/, not the raw folders. Otherwise the fake smoke and the 6k
    duplicates come back.
  - Never label inside data/raw/. Label copies, then bring the results back:
    reviewed labels go to data/manual_labels/, and unreviewed auto labels to
    data/interim/.
  - Auto-boxing only covers person and vehicle. face_covered in the theft
    datasets, three-wheelers, and any missed weapon or fire need a person to
    label them.
  - Answer the open decisions before you start (knives and swords, face_covered
    box size, helmets, small flames). Otherwise some images will need labelling
    twice.

  2. One zip or 11 zips?

  I'd use 11 zips, one per folder, with the folder names unchanged inside:

  ┌──────────────────┬─────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┐
  │                  │    11 separate zips     │         1 big zip          │
  ├──────────────────┼─────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │                  │ A failed upload only    │ One failure means          │
  │ Upload to Drive  │ repeats one folder      │ re-uploading everything    │
  │                  │                         │ (several GB)               │
  ├──────────────────┼─────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │                  │ Unzip, label, save and  │ Everything has to be       │
  │ Working in Colab │ review one dataset at a │ unzipped every session     │
  │                  │  time                   │                            │
  ├──────────────────┼─────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ Free Colab       │ You lose at most one    │ You can lose the whole run │
  │ disconnects      │ dataset's progress      │                            │
  ├──────────────────┼─────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ Review in        │ Already one task per    │ Has to be split again      │
  │ CVAT/Roboflow    │ dataset                 │ anyway                     │
  ├──────────────────┼─────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ Simplicity       │ Slightly more files to  │ One file                   │
  │                  │ manage                  │                            │
  └──────────────────┴─────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────┘

  Three Colab tips:
  - Never work directly on thousands of files inside mounted Drive. It's very
    slow. Copy the zip to Colab's local disk, unzip there, and zip the results
    back to Drive.
  - Check your Drive space. The free plan has 15 GB, and you'll store both the
    input zips and the output zips.
  - Keep the exact folder names, so results can be matched back to the right
    source.

  3. Removing duplicates before training: Colab or not?

  Colab can do it, since duplicate removal is ordinary CPU work. But I'd do it
  locally, before uploading:

  - It's already mostly built locally. The audit has the exact-duplicate checks,
    look-alike checks and copy groups for all 69,000 images, and the full scan
    took about 1 minute on your Mac. Phase 3 is designed to do the duplicate
    removal locally and record every decision in manifest.csv.
  - You upload less. Removing duplicates first shrinks the upload from about
    69,000 to about 37,000 images.
  - One process, one record. If duplicates were removed in Colab while the split
    and manifest are built locally, the two records could disagree and test
    images could leak into training.
  - Duplicate removal isn't "delete everything that looks similar". There are
    three different cases:
    - Exact duplicates and augmented copies: keep one copy.
    - Duplicates across datasets (Knife Detection ↔ Suspicious Activity, 6k ↔
      Fire-Smoke-CCTV): keep one copy.
    - Neighbouring video frames: these are different, valid images. Keep them,
      but group them, so they all land in the same split, and cap how many
      frames come from one clip.

    A simple similarity filter would wrongly delete good images. We saw this
    already: one 6k frame "matched" 25 different fire frames.

  Suggested order

  1. Answer the open decisions.
  2. Locally (Phase 3, steps 1–5): convert, remap, clean, remove duplicates (one
     copy per original) and apply the frame caps. This gives the ~37,000-image
     working set.
  3. Zip that working set one zip per dataset, then upload to Drive.
  4. Colab: auto-box person and vehicle, then save the results to Drive.
  5. Review in CVAT (or Roboflow) one dataset at a time, and hand-label the
     classes the model can't detect.
  6. Bring the labels back into data/manual_labels/. Locally (Phase 5):
     validate, then group-aware split, then build dataset.zip.
  7. Train in Colab.

  When you're ready, step 2 is the next thing I'd run, once you ask.

❯ If I map all 11 datasets into one dataset, removing duplicate images, is that
  okay? At this moment, I want your opinion on whether it is okay or not. If I
  have an issue, give me your suggestion.

⏺ My opinion: yes, it's okay, and it's the right plan

  Merging all 11 datasets into one dataset, with duplicates removed, is exactly
  what this project needs: one YOLO11 model trained on one dataset with the 7
  classes. It's also what CLAUDE.md describes. The merge itself is safe. The
  risks are in how it's done. These are the issues I'd watch, each with my
  suggestion:

  #: 1
  Possible issue: Inconsistent class mapping
  Why it matters: The same thing can end up labelled differently across sources.

  For example, a knife is weapon in Suspicious Activity, but Knife Detection
  and cctv are still undecided. Class ID 0 also means 8 different things across
   the 11 sources.
  My suggestion: Remap by class name with one class map, and answer the open
  decisions (especially knives and swords) before merging.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 2
  Possible issue: Removing duplicates too aggressively
  Why it matters: A simple "looks similar" filter deletes good images, such as
  neighbouring video frames with fire vs without fire.
  My suggestion: Remove only exact duplicates, augmented copies and true
  cross-source duplicates. Keep neighbouring frames, but group them so they
  land in the same split, and cap frames per clip.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 3
  Possible issue: Choosing which duplicate to keep
  Why it matters: The same image can be labelled differently in two sources (for

  example, Knife Detection vs Suspicious Activity, or 6k vs Fire-Smoke-CCTV).
  My suggestion: Keep the copy with the more complete labels, the one covering
  more of the 7 classes. Don't combine labels from two sources automatically.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 4
  Possible issue: Merging doesn't fix missing boxes
  Why it matters: About 46% of images still have unboxed people or vehicles. The

  merged set would carry those gaps.
  My suggestion: Order: merge and remove duplicates → auto-box → review → split.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 5
  Possible issue: Reusing the old train/valid/test splits
  Why it matters: The original splits already leak: the same clips appear in
  more
  than one split.
  My suggestion: Ignore them. Make one new split after duplicate removal
  (70/15/15, seed 42, group-aware, every class in every split).
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 6
  Possible issue: Losing track of where each image came from
  Why it matters: You need the source for reviewing, licences, privacy
  (parking),
  and re-running the build.
  My suggestion: Keep the source id in each file name (<source_id>__<name>) and
  in manifest.csv.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 7
  Possible issue: Imbalance after merging
  Why it matters: About 20× (vehicle 10,778 vs loitering 551).
  My suggestion: Cap the large classes (about 3,000 each, as I suggested), and
  add more loitering and tampering data later.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 8
  Possible issue: Too many background images
  Why it matters: The 6k "normal" images could dominate.
  My suggestion: Keep background at 5–10% of train, and only after auto-boxing
  confirms the images are empty.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 9
  Possible issue: Mixed licences
  Why it matters: The parking set is "Private", and fire-dataset's licence is
  unknown.
  My suggestion: Fine for your university project. Keep the merged dataset
  private; don't publish it.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 10
  Possible issue: Changing the original data
  Why it matters: You'd lose the ability to rebuild or fix anything.
  My suggestion: Build the merge with scripts into data/merged/, keep data/raw/
  untouched, and make it re-runnable.

  Bottom line: merge and de-duplicate, yes. But do it as a scripted, logged
  build (Phase 3), with the class map decided first, careful duplicate rules,
  the source kept in every file name, and the split made last. Done that way,
  merging is the right step. Done as a quick "copy everything into one folder
  and delete similar-looking images", it would cause silent label conflicts and
  train/test leakage.

  The merged dataset is built and verified. Verdict: READY WITH WARNINGS. You

  Where everything is:
  - The dataset: data/merged/, with train/, valid/ and test/ (each holding
    images/ and labels/), plus data.yaml and manifest.csv.
  - For Colab: data/export/dataset.zip (1.95 GB). It has train/ valid/ test/

    of removed and excluded images in reports/03_*.csv.

  What went in and out:
  - Input: 65,544 images (the 11 datasets, with the two cleaned copies).
  - Duplicates removed: 23,319 (22,343 augmented copies, 20 byte-identical
    files, 956 pictures that appear in two datasets).
  - Excluded by your logged Suspicious Activity rules: 4,581 (2,383 Smoking-only
    images, 2,198 frames over the 150-per-clip cap).

  How the classes were mapped:
  - Thief detection:
    - human and human in hurry → person
    - car → vehicle
    - gun and weapon → weapon
    - human_brakingdoor and human_lockunlocking → tampering (break-in attempts,
      like Surveillance breaking_in)
    - face hiding → face_covered

    pedestrian is boxed (about 7 per image), so mapping it to loitering would
    teach the model that everyone walking past is loitering. It adds person data
    but doesn't improve the loitering imbalance: loitering is still 576 images.

  How the classes cover what you asked for

  behaviour boxes need hand labelling.

  Steps in Colab

  1. In Drive, create a folder component4 and upload dataset.zip into it.
  2. Open https://colab.research.google.com → File → Upload notebook → choose
     the .ipynb file.
  3. Runtime → Change runtime type → T4 GPU → Save.
  4. Run the cells from top to bottom (Shift + Enter):
     - Step 1: settings.
     - Step 2: checks the GPU (must say CUDA available: True) and installs
       YOLO11 (Ultralytics 8.4.174).
     - Step 3: connects Drive (click Allow), then copies and unzips the dataset.
     - Step 4: fixes data.yaml.
     - Step 5: adds the missing person/vehicle boxes (about 20–40 minutes, my
       estimate).
     - Step 6: shows 12 example images (red = added box), then saves
       dataset_autolabelled.zip to Drive.
     - Step 7: checks the dataset.
     - Step 8: trains yolo11s, up to 60 epochs, saving to Drive after every
       epoch.
     - Step 9: test-split scores per class, plus sample predictions.
     - Step 10: exports best.pt and an ONNX copy.
  5. If Colab disconnects: set the GPU again, then Runtime → Run all. The
     notebook reuses the auto-labelled zip and resumes training. I estimate 6–10
     minutes per epoch on a free T4, so 60 epochs will probably need more than
     one session.

  Things to know

  - The added boxes aren't reviewed yet, so treat this model as a first version.
    Afterwards, bring dataset_autolabelled.zip back for Phase 5: review the
    boxes, then train again.
  - Expect weak loitering scores: 576 images from one source, and the class
    imbalance is 21.6×.
  - Run time: the build now has a fake-fire step (03c_remove_fake_fire.py).
    python scripts/run_pipeline.py rebuilds everything, and the decisions are
    logged in docs/decision_log.md.

❯ Why does this training process stop while training the model?
  I just want the reason; if I want a solution, I will tell you.

⏺ First, this isn't the training step. It stopped during Step 5
  (auto-labelling), after about 14,432 of the 28,002 train images, before
  training began.

  The reason: the Colab runtime lost its connection, so the code stopped
  running. It wasn't an error in the code. Three signs in your screenshot point
  to this:

  1. No error message. A crash inside the code always prints a red traceback
     under the cell. The output just stops.
  2. "Saving failed since 19:27" next to the title means the notebook page lost
     its connection to Google's servers at 19:27.
  3. The RAM and Disk bars on the right are empty, and the cell shows ▶ instead
     of a spinning stop icon. No machine is attached any more, so the runtime
     was disconnected or shut down.

  Common reasons free Colab disconnects:
  - the laptop went to sleep, or the browser tab was in the background for a
    long time;
  - the internet connection dropped, even briefly;
  - the free-tier limits: sessions are cut after a period of inactivity or when
    the free GPU allowance runs out.

  One other possibility is that the runtime ran out of RAM. In that case Colab
  usually shows a pop-up saying "Your session crashed after using all available
  RAM". If you saw that message, that was the cause instead.

  Everything Step 5 wrote so far was on Colab's temporary disk (/content). It
  isn't saved to Drive until Step 6, so that progress was lost when the runtime
  disconnected.

  8.
  I just want your answer, suggestions, or opinions only.

⏺ Yes, you can continue next time from where it stopped. It resumes from the
  last completed epoch, though, not from an "early stopping stage". Your
  notebook saves training to Google Drive
  (MyDrive/component4/runs/c4_yolo11s/weights/last.pt), and Ultralytics updates
  last.pt after every finished epoch. Only the epoch that was running when the
  session ended is lost.

  What "patience=20" actually means

  It doesn't switch on after 20 epochs. It means: stop early if the validation
  score hasn't improved for 20 epochs in a row. For example, if the best result
  was at epoch 25 and epochs 26–45 are all worse, training stops at epoch 45
  instead of running to 60. Until that happens, training simply continues.
  Resuming doesn't undo this: the best result so far is stored in the
  checkpoint. The count of epochs without improvement may start again from the
  resume point, but that only makes it stop slightly later, not earlier.

  How to resume next time

  1. Check in Drive that MyDrive/component4/runs/c4_yolo11s/weights/last.pt
     exists. If it doesn't, the first epoch never finished, and training starts
     from the beginning.
     - results.csv in the same c4_yolo11s folder shows how many epochs are done:
       one row per epoch.
  2. Check that MyDrive/component4/dataset_autolabelled.zip exists. If it does,
     Step 3 loads it and Steps 5–6 are skipped. If it doesn't, Step 5 has to run
     again, which uses up a lot of your session time.
  3. Set the GPU to T4, then Runtime → Run all. Step 8 finds last.pt and prints
     "Resuming from …".
  4. Don't change RUN_NAME, RUNS_DIR or the runs folder in Drive. Changing
     EPOCHS or BATCH in Step 1 has no effect when resuming, because the run
     keeps its original settings.

  What your screenshot shows

  - The real limit is free GPU time, not the code. The Resources panel says "You
    currently have zero compute units… this runtime may last up to 1 hour 30
    minutes." Each session will be short, and once the free GPU allowance runs
    out, Colab may refuse a GPU until the next day.
  - Memory is fine: RAM 6.9 / 12.7 GB, GPU 7.8 / 15 GB, so the settings aren't
    causing the stops.
  - Expect many sessions: at roughly 6–10 minutes per epoch (my estimate), 1.5
    hours is about 9–15 epochs, so 60 epochs needs about 4–7 sessions. Early
    stopping may end it sooner.

  Suggestions (only if you want them)

  - Keep the laptop awake and the Colab tab in front during training. Sleep or a
    background tab makes disconnects more likely.
  - Kaggle Notebooks (kaggle.com) are also free and usually give about 30 GPU
    hours per week, with longer sessions (about 9–12 hours). That fits your
    zero-cost rule, and the same notebook works with small path changes.
  - Fewer epochs: if you'd rather finish within fewer sessions, you could start
    a new run with a smaller EPOCHS (e.g. 30) and a new RUN_NAME. The trade-off
    is a somewhat less accurate model. Resuming the current run is usually
    better than restarting.
